But even then, I’d still hear that supervisor-type voice, or I’d feel like the old ticketing system was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the voices were still there but you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve had stretches that lasted a few weeks where I wasn’t really depressed, or at least my mood was closer to normal and I was getting things done. But the voice was still there sometimes—the supervisor voice—or I’d still feel like the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when other people said your speech was hard to follow or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, no one’s really told me I wasn’t making sense. Sometimes I lose my train of thought or have to pause, but I haven’t noticed people saying my speech is hard to follow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I was irritated or a little more keyed up, but not like unusually high or euphoric. I’ll pace around my apartment and rub my hands together, like I’m trying to shake off stress from a bad call.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voice stayed present for at least two weeks while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. My sleep can be off sometimes, but I haven’t noticed needing way less sleep and still feeling great or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly hearing things. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name, or saying “too slow.” But my laptop will be closed, and there’s no phone ringing or anything. I don’t really see things, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s been like that for a long time now—about two years, maybe a little over. Most days I feel depressed for most of the day. It lets up here and there, but only for short stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear those voices at times when you are not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s happened. I’ve had a few stretches, maybe a couple weeks at a time, where my mood felt more normal and I was getting things done. But even then, I’d still hear that supervisor-type voice, or I’d feel like the old ticketing system was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the voices were still there but you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve had stretches that lasted a few weeks where I wasn’t really depressed, or at least my mood was closer to normal and I was getting things done. But the voice was still there sometimes—the supervisor voice—or I’d still feel like the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when other people said your speech was hard to follow or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, no one’s really told me I wasn’t making sense. Sometimes I lose my train of thought or have to pause, but I haven’t noticed people saying my speech is hard to follow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I was irritated or a little more keyed up, but not like unusually high or euphoric. Nothing that really stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. During those times, I just don’t really enjoy things. I used to like sketching, making coffee notes, going on weekend hikes… those made me feel like myself. But when I’m depressed, I’ll open the notebook or get my boots on and it’s like there’s nothing there. I don’t feel pulled toward it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been going on a lot longer than two weeks, honestly. I’ve been feeling depressed most days for a little over two years now, with maybe short stretches where it lets up a bit, but it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a long period where you felt emotionally flat or had very little facial expression?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My friends have told me I sound kind of flat now, like I’m hard to read. Even when I do care about what they’re saying, I usually just give short answers, and I tend to avoid making plans.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, have you had stretches where your body felt almost frozen or stuck, like it was very hard to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There are times where I feel really slowed down, like I’m moving through water. People notice it — I’ll take forever to answer a question or even just get up from a chair.\n\nOther times it’s the opposite, where I can’t sit still. I’ll pace around my apartment and rub my hands together, like I’m trying to shake off stress from a bad call.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voice stayed present for at least two weeks while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been a few stretches, like a few weeks at a time, where my mood was more normal and I was getting things done. But even then I’d still hear that supervisor voice, or I’d feel really sure the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you ever feel worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I get stuck replaying little mistakes, especially from work or contract jobs. Like if I was slow to reply to something, it feels like I let everyone down, and then I start thinking maybe I deserved to be fired.\n\nDoctor: During these depressed periods, have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve felt really low and overwhelmed, but I haven’t had thoughts of killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and movement changes, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Most of the time since all this started, the depression stuff has kind of been there in the background — feeling sad, guilty, not interested in things, and my sleep being off. Some weeks are definitely lighter than others, but it’s been there more often than not.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when the voice or the feeling of being flagged was there, but you were not depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it was a full month exactly. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where my mood felt more normal and I was getting things done, but the supervisor voice was still there sometimes, or I still felt pretty sure the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: At any point, did the voice or the feeling of being flagged stay present continuously for a full month?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, the voice and that feeling like I was being tracked or flagged have been around off and on for about a year and eight months. It wasn’t only when I was at my lowest mood-wise. There were times I wasn’t as depressed, but that stuff was still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But the voice was still there sometimes—the supervisor voice—or I’d still feel like the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when other people said your speech was hard to follow or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, no one’s really told me I wasn’t making sense. Sometimes I lose my train of thought or have to pause, but I haven’t noticed people saying my speech is hard to follow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I was irritated or a little more keyed up, but not like unusually high or euphoric. Nothing that really stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. During those times, I just don’t really enjoy things. I used to like sketching, making coffee notes, going on weekend hikes… those made me feel like myself. But when I’m depressed, I’ll open the notebook or get my boots on and it’s like there’s nothing there. I don’t feel pulled toward it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been going on a lot longer than two weeks, honestly. I’ve been feeling depressed most days for a little over two years now, with maybe short stretches where it lets up a bit, but it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a long period where you felt emotionally flat or had very little facial expression?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My friends have told me I sound kind of flat now, like I’m hard to read. Even when I do care about what they’re saying, I usually just give short answers, and I tend to avoid making plans.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, have you had stretches where your body felt almost frozen or stuck, like it was very hard to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There are times where I feel really slowed down, like I’m moving through water. I’ll pace around my apartment and rub my hands together, like I’m trying to shake off stress from a bad call.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voice stayed present for at least two weeks while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been a few stretches, like a few weeks at a time, where my mood was more normal and I was getting things done. But even then I’d still hear that supervisor voice, or I’d feel really sure the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you ever feel worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I get stuck replaying little mistakes, especially from work or contract jobs. Like if I was slow to reply to something, it feels like I let everyone down, and then I start thinking maybe I deserved to be fired.\n\nDoctor: During these depressed periods, have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve felt really low and overwhelmed, but I haven’t had thoughts of killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and movement changes, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Most of the time since all this started, the depression stuff has kind of been there in the background — feeling sad, guilty, not interested in things, and my sleep being off. Some weeks are definitely lighter than others, but it’s been there more often than not.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when the voice or the feeling of being flagged was there, but you were not depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it was a full month exactly. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where my mood felt more normal and I was getting things done, but the supervisor voice was still there sometimes, or I still felt pretty sure the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: At any point, did the voice or the feeling of being flagged stay present continuously for a full month?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, the voice and that feeling like I was being tracked or flagged have been around off and on for about a year and eight months. It wasn’t only when I was at my lowest mood-wise. There were times I wasn’t as depressed, but that stuff was still there.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least one month when the voice or the feeling of being flagged was present while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was definitely a full month. I don’t really see things, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s been like that for a long time now—about two years, maybe a little over. Most days I feel depressed for most of the day. It lets up here and there, but only for short stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear those voices at times when you are not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s happened. I’ve had a few stretches, maybe a couple weeks at a time, where my mood felt more normal and I was getting things done. But even then, I’d still hear that supervisor-type voice, or I’d feel like the old ticketing system was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the voices were still there but you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve had stretches that lasted a few weeks where I wasn’t really depressed, or at least my mood was closer to normal and I was getting things done. But the voice was still there sometimes—the supervisor voice—or I’d still feel like the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when other people said your speech was hard to follow or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, no one’s really told me I wasn’t making sense. Sometimes I lose my train of thought or have to pause, but I haven’t noticed people saying my speech is hard to follow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I was irritated or a little more keyed up, but not like unusually high or euphoric. Nothing that really stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. During those times, I just don’t really enjoy things. I used to like sketching, making coffee notes, going on weekend hikes… those made me feel like myself. But when I’m depressed, I’ll open the notebook or get my boots on and it’s like there’s nothing there. I don’t feel pulled toward it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been going on a lot longer than two weeks, honestly. I’ve been feeling depressed most days for a little over two years now, with maybe short stretches where it lets up a bit, but it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a long period where you felt emotionally flat or had very little facial expression?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My friends have told me I sound kind of flat now, like I’m hard to read. Even when I do care about what they’re saying, I usually just give short answers, and I tend to avoid making plans.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, have you had stretches where your body felt almost frozen or stuck, like it was very hard to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There are times where I feel really slowed down, like I’m moving through water. People notice it — I’ll take forever to answer a question or even just get up from a chair.\n\nOther times it’s the opposite, where I can’t sit still. I’ll pace around my apartment and rub my hands together, like I’m trying to shake off stress from a bad call.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voice stayed present for at least two weeks while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been a few stretches, like a few weeks at a time, where my mood was more normal and I was getting things done. But even then I’d still hear that supervisor voice, or I’d feel really sure the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you ever feel worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I get stuck replaying little mistakes, especially from work or contract jobs. Like if I was slow to reply to something, it feels like I let everyone down, and then I start thinking maybe I deserved to be fired.\n\nDoctor: During these depressed periods, have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve felt really low and overwhelmed, but I haven’t had thoughts of killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and movement changes, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Most of the time since all this started, the depression stuff has kind of been there in the background — feeling sad, guilty, not interested in things, and my sleep being off. Some weeks are definitely lighter than others, but it’s been there more often than not.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when the voice or the feeling of being flagged was there, but you were not depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it was a full month exactly. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where my mood felt more normal and I was getting things done, but the supervisor voice was still there sometimes, or I still felt pretty sure the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: At any point, did the voice or the feeling of being flagged stay present continuously for a full month?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, the voice and that feeling like I was being tracked or flagged have been around off and on for about a year and eight months. It wasn’t only when I was at my lowest mood-wise. There were times I wasn’t as depressed, but that stuff was still there.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least one month when the voice or the feeling of being flagged was present while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was definitely a full month. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where my mood was more normal and I was functioning better, but I was still hearing the supervisor voice sometimes or feeling like the old ticketing system was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when you were not depressed, but the voice or the feeling of being flagged was still there?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure it was a full month. What I do know is I’ve had a few-week stretch where my mood was closer to normal and I was getting things done, but I still heard the supervisor voice sometimes, or felt like the old ticketing software was flagging me."
